## Current handoff — read first

**Standing UI scope — user clarified 2026-10-02.** The viewer is a local development test harness only. Add or change UI solely when necessary to exercise or inspect engine behavior; do not pursue product UI, visual polish or feature parity. It will **not be packaged or distributed alongside the library**. Viewer scripts, launcher and Tk/Pillow/decoder runtimes stay outside the library installation. Prior optional viewer-panel suggestions are deferred unless a concrete testing need requires them. Prioritize engine implementation, correctness, performance and library integration.

**Purpose and update rule.** This section is the continuation point for Codex on any machine. At the end of **every implementation run**, update the snapshot, verification, open issues, and next action here in this same file; append one concise entry to the run log below. Record actual commands/results and the last code commit. Keep the detailed roadmap, dependency ledger, and ADRs below consistent. Do not create a separate handoff or assume that a prior chat is available. If a run ends before verification, mark it unverified rather than implying completion. During an ongoing conversation, carry forward verified context and inspect specific files as needed; reread this handoff only when context is missing or the checkout changes. Update it at implementation checkpoints, not for each user message.

**Objective qualification — user clarified 2026-10-02.** Personal taste/preferred edited references are not required and must not define or block the engine. Select documented engineering contracts under this plan; use independent mathematical truth, declared color behavior, signed/headroom handling, numerical continuity, artifact checks, replay/integration and performance evidence. Photographic/profile references evaluate objective properties; editor matching is a separate explicitly selected compatibility target. Broader corpus/profile qualification remains open without blocking bounded implementation. The earlier request for personal reference edits was unnecessary. [Qualification scope](../COLOR_CONTROL_QUALIFICATION.md).

**Standing performance requirement — user requested 2026-10-01.** As work proceeds, record observed slowdowns and tag plausible improvement points in the [performance log](#performance-observations-and-improvement-log), including rendering, copying/allocations, cache, queueing and build/test/tool delays when material. Capture evidence while the context is fresh: triggering input/request, affected symbol/file, hardware/build/settings, elapsed time or resource counters, baseline if available, reproduction command/report path and next focused diagnostic. Use stable `PERF-###` IDs; distinguish **measured cost**, **confirmed regression**, **hypothesis**, **unmeasured gap** and **resolved**. Timing without a comparable baseline is not a confirmed regression. Link entries from the run log/handoff and use a `PERF-###:` code comment only where a specific non-obvious cost/constraint merits a durable pointer. Do not scatter speculative TODOs, add hot-loop logging, collect sensitive image contents or run expensive full profiling after every small change. Preserve relevant measurements, note confounders and record before/after correctness and memory tradeoffs when resolving an entry. Logging a possible improvement does not authorize expanding the current milestone; carry deferred items forward.

**Feature-first priority — user clarified2026-10-03.** Finish implementing the original build plan before targeting performance fixes. Continue low-overhead slowdown logging in [PERFORMANCE_ISSUES.md](../PERFORMANCE_ISSUES.md),with stable IDs,reproduction/evidence and deferred improvement hypotheses. Defer dedicated allocation/stage profiling,performance investigations,benchmark expansion and optimizations until the feature build is complete. Continue the objective correctness,domain/schema/replay/ROI/mip/cache/jobs/history and installed-consumer checks required to complete each feature. Existing performance/resource findings and unresolved qualification rows are retained;no new headline checklist rows or UI scope.

- **Repository:** standalone librawops,main,HEAD/latest code commit85c989d,Git root`C:\Users\tylle\Documents\librawops\librawops`. Current56/56 default,30/30 core,31/31 LittleCMS;native/seven Python clarity cases,16,920 exact prototype outputs/2,700 native tile comparisons,144 exact camera references/twelve boards,viewer9/9 and adapter10/10. Before-clarity contract/binaries/nine sources preserved,prior grading/LUT evidence archived/bound. Local changes,no commit/push. UI/dependency/default/source identity/Menon/policy/NR unchanged.
- **Mission:** reusable, application-independent C++20 non-destructive RAW and raster editing library with optional native Python bindings. Do not work on or integrate with ImageTriage. The no-copyleft rule applies to **every shipped component**. The core accepts decoded uint16 Bayer data and must remain decoder-free. A permissive RAW-file decoder path is a release gate, not a prerequisite for current library work.
- **Current code maturity:** Phase 0 evidence, Phase 1 typed/color foundations, Phase 2 graph/format work and bounded Phase 3 RAW preview/session work are **In Progress**. This is a prototype, not a production-ready Camera Raw replacement or a stable C++ ABI. The edit manifest builds an immutable executable DAG for existing unary point/color operations, two-input scene-linear linear mix, exact quarter-turn/flip orientation, plus a bounded neighborhood reference (`rawengine.box_blur`), integer crop (`rawengine.crop`) and nearest/bilinear/area resize (`rawengine.resize`). Canonical source fingerprints are verified at binding; optional process-local LRU caching reuses unchanged upstream tiles across revisions, including either unchanged mix branch; tile-boundary cancellation, a bounded priority request queue and group-scoped viewport supersession exist. `Node::input_region()` declares native blur and RAW-demosaic halos, and `input_region_level()` defines reduced blur mapping; `ExecutableEditGraph::required_source_regions()` composes both through unary/two-input paths and unions shared-source footprints. Typed `RenderRequest` carries viewport, tile size, mip and quality through renderer/scheduler entry points. Scene-linear raster sources support direct clipped 2×2 mip 1 and 4×4 mip 2 previews; exposure, working-space conversion and box blur run after reduction with per-level cache identity. The explicit linear-sRGB conversion, tone and encoded-sRGB output chain also supports mip 1/2 preview after source reduction. Other reduced nodes reject requests. Native/reduced crop and quarter-turn/flip coordinate mapping, native/reduced resize sampling/ROI planning and Python async output-tile progress are present. Bounded arbitrary-angle rotation and explicit-canvas projective mapping execute; bounded scale-aware cubic resize executes;production resampling qualification, general compositing, brush/parametric/range/feather masks, source-version/binary asset persistence and general core progress remain absent. Python RasterSession owns one immutable source; RasterGraphSession owns up to 64 explicit source IDs with atomic replacement; both share a bounded cache across synchronous and asynchronous recipe or saved-manifest renders. A deterministic Photoshop-raster reference harness exists, but **no Adobe reference measurements** exist on this machine; do not claim Adobe compatibility. Typed scalar mask sources/algebra and named masked RGB adjustment now execute in explicit format4; Python mixed coverage sources and explicit scalar session/history methods now execute; scalar/RGB jobs share one queue budget and group namespace.
- **Source and graph:** `RawEngine.hpp/.cpp` define validated RAW/raster sources, typed descriptors, existing operation nodes, legacy `GraphRecipe`/`ImageGraph`, and tiled `Renderer` with typed `RenderRequest` overloads. RAW has immutable default bilinear v1 and opt-in Menon base v1, signed normalization, site levels, CFA phase and active area. `RasterSourceNode` provides native final/preview pixels and mip 1/2 scene-linear previews by averaging direct 2×2/4×4 source footprints with actual sample counts at edges. ResizeNode maps native output centers to edge-clamped nearest/bilinear samples or integrates source-cell overlaps with area filtering; its mip-1/2 previews average native resized float32 output; source footprints compose through crop/blur/mix. CropNode rebases native coordinates and anchors mip-1/2 reduction at the crop origin; Node::input_level switches its upstream work to native pixels. The executable graph retains original source_bounds and transformed output_bounds plus per-stage input extents. Exposure and working-space conversion process reduced scene-linear pixels; `BoxBlurNode` measures radius/halo in reduced pixels and crops to output. Optional LittleCMS accepts owned ICC-encoded uint16 RGB samples and converts requested tiles. `SourceFingerprint.cpp` and `Sha256.cpp` compute canonical v1 digests from rendering metadata and visible samples; immutable RAW/raster objects memoize them. `EditGraph.hpp/.cpp` define format-v2/v3/v4 JSON state with pinned RAW reconstruction identity, verified source bindings, executable unary/two-input DAG construction, optional shared bounded LRU tile caching with mip/quality key fields, and level-aware `required_source_regions()` for supported unary/two-input paths; singular helpers reject outputs depending on multiple source IDs. Cached wrappers pass levels and reduction-anchor mappings to underlying nodes. RAW demosaic/WB and camera-linear exposure execute natively before a bounded camera-to-working anchor averages calibrated float32 RGB; subsequent supported scene-linear edits and sRGB output process reduced pixels. Native sensor origins are retained for final requests, reduced output starts at zero, and source footprints include active-area offsets and the clipped native demosaic halo. ICC import/display, unmanaged tone/output and legacy fixed-chain manifests remain native final. `TileScheduler.hpp/.cpp` add a process-local priority queue and `submit_latest(group, ...)` to replace queued work and cancel obsolete running work at the next tile boundary; the scheduler accepts typed reduced requests and `Renderer` polls cancellation between tiles. Product file codecs, broader mip/quality processing, production geometry beyond bounded rotation/projective tap planning, general compositing and brush/parametric/range/feather mask processing remain absent. tools/render_raster_fixture.py is a diagnostic bridge for pinned sRGB TIFF fixtures, not a product codec. `MaskOps.hpp/.cpp` add typed scalar nodes, invert/combine and masked RGB; graph format4 binds coverage sources and named edges, shares typed cache payloads, and plans mixed source-ID footprints. `CoverageDelivery.hpp/.cpp` stream and assemble validated scalar tiles; TileScheduler now admits typed coverage futures on the shared worker pool. `BrushMask.hpp/.cpp` add copied ordered paint/erase settings, exported scalar replay and CoverageBrushNode; rawengine.mask_brush forces native Final upstream before direct preview reduction. `ParametricMask.hpp/.cpp` add copied linear/radial/polygon scalar masks and strict saved types, each replaying native Final input before direct preview reduction.
- **Color/output:** white balance gains and linear exposure precede an optional caller-calibrated camera RGB→XYZ D50 matrix. The graph supports linear ProPhoto/D50 and Rec.2020/D65; **ProPhoto/D50 is now the canonical default**, with explicit typed `WorkingSpaceConvertNode` between the two and no clipping. This is an architectural choice, **not measured Adobe equivalence**. The default `LegacyBounded` output is bounded but unmanaged. `SrgbPreview` converts scene-linear RGB to linear sRGB, applies the prototype tone curve, hard-clips and sRGB-encodes. `IccDisplay` accepts a thread-safe transform, validates output, and tags tiles with the output-profile digest; optional `LittleCmsBackend.hpp/.cpp` now supplies both RGB display/output ICC conversion and uint16 RGB ICC raster import with intent/BPC. Monitor discovery, soft proof, alpha and general export color are absent. Python exposes one-shot native-final RAW plus calibrated RAW/raster mip-1/2 sRGB rendering, persistent RawSession/RasterSession source/cache ownership and RasterGraphSession multi-raster ownership; native rendering releases the GIL. Python submit/submit_latest return cancellable RenderJob objects with timed repeatable results and output tile progress; Python ICC transforms remain absent; one-source and multi-owned-source saved-manifest rendering are available.
- **Dependency boundary:** The default build uses C++ standard library plus optional OpenMP and CPython C API. `RAWENGINE_WITH_LCMS=ON` opts into the audited LittleCMS **2.19.1 core-only** static build; its exact archive SHA-256 is pinned in CMake. GPL fast-float and threaded plugins, tools, tests and optional codecs are disabled. The staged Windows install contains no GPL plugin binary or source; LittleCMS's installed `lcms2_plugin.h` is MIT-licensed, not the GPL plugin implementation. Keep future transitive, platform, binary and notice audits in the [dependency ledger](#dependency-and-license-decisions).
- **Key files:** `EditGraph.hpp/.cpp` and `tests/edit_graph_tests.cpp` cover manifest parsing/migration, runtime graph validation, RAW/raster parity, canonical fingerprints, cache reuse/eviction/clear and mip/quality separation, typed native render parity and unsupported-level rejection, cancellation, scheduled priority/concurrent ROI requests, group supersession, box-blur halo/tiled/full parity with radius invalidation, chained blur/source-region planning, and RAW demosaic halo planning. `tests/lcms_tests.cpp` covers ICC output/import round trips, source/output policy binding and encoded-sample fingerprints. `tests/python_raw_session_tests.py` covers owned RAW metadata/source identity, recipes/cache, saved graphs/jobs/history and queued source lifetimes. `tests/raw_preview_tests.cpp` and `tests/python_raw_preview_tests.py` cover calibrated RAW native/reduced references, all Bayer patterns/phases, active-area coordinates, odd/narrow edges, padded site levels, composed geometry/blur/branch footprints and cache revisions. `tests/core_tests.cpp` covers RAW metadata, signed headroom, color matrices, the 347-patch working-space grid, ROI/descriptors and mock ICC boundary. `tests/smoke.py` covers legacy native Python entry points; `tests/python_preview_tests.py` covers reduced requests/crop, RasterSession ownership/revision reuse, budgets and concurrent cache clearing; `tests/python_async_tests.py` covers job waits, cancellation/supersession, queue limits, progress and lifetimes; `tests/python_manifest_tests.py` covers saved graph identity/replay/branching, cache reuse, strict requests, job groups and ownership; tests/python_multi_source_tests.py covers independent-image mix/geometry, source replacement/cache reuse, footprint mapping and coherent job/source snapshots; tests/edit_history_tests.cpp and tests/python_history_tests.py cover immutable revision history, retention, replay, comparisons, source pinning, cache reuse and job lifetimes. `tools/reference_harness.py` and `tests/reference_harness_tests.py` generate/compare pinned ICC-tagged TIFF fixtures without an Adobe claim. The ICC test asset is `tests/reference/profiles/sRGB2014.icc` (SHA-256 `384b832de3412066743b52a75ee906b6fb9d9e09e936fc2c43223815c6e0a`, [ICC redistribution terms](https://registry.color.org/rgb-registry/srgbprofiles)). Five C++ bench programs measure synthetic rendering, working-space conversion, ICC tile cost, cache/revision cost and source-only Menon reconstruction; bench/raster_session_benchmark.py now measures one-shot/cold/warm/late-tone Python previews. `README.md` is the consumer API guide. This file is the **single** living architecture/plan/handoff document.

### Menon guarded base experiment and rejection — 2026-10-01

The [separate guarded scalar variant](../../tools/raw_menon_guard.py) implements the preregistered base-only sign guard under `librawops.menon.curvature-sign-guard.scalar-research`, version 1. All **2,368** pairs retain exact observed values/quantization. Guarded v1 has **128** analytical failures (64 neutral affine + 64 chromatic affine), **2,848** failed regression checks across **800** cases, and **14/24** improvement gates. Relative to original base's 3,840 checks/896 cases/16 gates, the guard improves every chromatic RGB mean and changes 1,664 rendered buffers, but loses four neutral hard-edge gates while gaining two chromatic 1/8 sine gates. ### Ordered sequence recorded before the audit — 2026-09-30

Work through these bounded checkpoints in order:

1. **Audit the analytical error/quantization budget before another algorithm.** The current bound is the maximum sensor-site half-step, `0.5 / (white - black) + 1e-6`. Cross-channel correction can propagate or amplify differently quantized sites; distinguish an implementation defect from an analytical tolerance that does not cover that propagation. Derive the allowance from the equations and site-level quantization, using exactly encoded and deliberately quantized fixtures, while retaining observed-site fidelity and signed/headroom requirements. Do not weaken thresholds simply to admit Hamilton–Adams. Any justified contract revision must be explicit and versioned, with the original policy and results retained.
2. **Investigate Menon DDFAPD.** Obtain/check primary equations and exact source/license/patent provenance, then specify all staged dependencies, composed halo, active-area borders, thin-image behavior, ties and precision before implementation. Admission remains open.
3. **Build an independent scalar study and compare all 2,368 frozen fixtures.** Preserve fixture identities and the bilinear baseline; report improvements and regressions under the original policy and any separately justified/versioned analytical contract. A completed evaluation is not replacement acceptance.
4. **Admit representative real-camera crops and inspect them.** Complete rights/source/decode, camera/profile/WB/view and ROI records; assess false color, zippering, moiré, detail, noise, signed shadows and highlights. Keep camera observations separate from synthetic ground-truth measurements. There are currently zero admitted real-camera assets.
5. **Port only a passing, admitted candidate to C++.** Give it a new immutable algorithm/version, preserve bilinear v1, and verify staged halo/borders, full/tile/ROI parity, cache/history/replay, native-before-reduction previews, allocations and performance before replacement acceptance.

Hamilton–Adams scalar v1 remains **rejected under the current policy**. The quantization counterexample motivates an audit, not a finding that the implementation is defective or an acceptance reversal; its chromatic regression failures remain material even if an analytical allowance is revised. Continue with small, reviewable milestones. No commit or push has been authorized.

### Scalar Hamilton–Adams quality disposition — 2026-09-30

`tools/raw_ha_reference.py` implements original research math from visually inspected original US5629734 equation figures (PDF pages 9–10, printed pages 5–8), with symmetric exact ties, normalized float32 observations, binary64 predictors/classifiers, a float32 green stage and float32 final missing channels, observed-site preservation and three-pixel bilinear output borders. Strict integer per-site metadata and a one-million-sample fixture budget bound this scalar tooling. Its full-active-plane green storage is an oracle choice, not the proposed native tile allocation design. No third-party implementation was copied, native algorithm registered, dependency installed or renderer changed. Shipment/jurisdiction admission remains separate and unresolved.

The evaluator regenerates/identifies the complete preserved corpus, reuses existing metric reducers and the frozen policy, and emits **research schema 1**, reference/helper source hashes, generator/formula/domain provenance and measured gate results. It does not forge source bindings/manifests/native tile or cache/history evidence; `native_replacement_accepted` is always false. All 2,368 cases retain observed fidelity and quantization, but 144 analytical cases fail (64 neutral affine, 64 chromatic affine, 16 neutral-mid flat), and 2,784 score/scope regressions affect 832 cases. All sixteen neutral edge/detail RGB/chroma improvement gates pass (roughly 47–72% RGB improvement); eight chromatic gates fail, including 22–25% worse sine RMSE. **Do not promote this scalar v1 to native code or loosen policy thresholds to admit it.**

Selected synthetic views cannot overrule full camera-linear metrics or establish camera/perceptual/Adobe quality.

### Paired policy and versioned RAW demosaic continuation — 2026-09-30

`RawDemosaicIdentity` contains stable algorithm `rawengine.bilinear` and processing version 1. `RawUnpackNode(RawImage)` still delegates to that exact path; the new overload validates an explicit identity and reports it through `Node::raw_demosaic_identity()`. Reconstruction math and one-pixel halo are unchanged. Unknown algorithms/versions reject; no fallback or second backend exists.

Format 3 requires a `demosaic` object on every decoded-Bayer source and forbids it on raster sources. Formats 1/2 forbid explicit policy and permanently imply bilinear version 1. The format-1 reader retains its existing raster-space migration to format 2. Raster recipes continue exporting format 2. Python `RawSession(..., demosaic=None)` defaults to bilinear version 1; an explicit dict requires exactly string `algorithm` and integer `processing_version`, is copied, and cannot change through recipe options. RAW exports use format 3, and source_info returns a copied policy. Sample fingerprints remain unchanged: policy is a separate reconstruction identity.

Binding normalizes absent legacy RAW policy to bilinear version 1 and checks the runtime node's reported policy as well as fingerprint/bounds/color. RAW nodes without a policy identity reject. Cache source signatures and history source comparisons use that same canonical effective policy; implicit/explicit bilinear reuse tiles, mixed-format history commits/save/restore render identical pixels, and unsupported policy cannot publish a revision or queue a job. Histories/jobs retain their existing immutable source-node ownership. These public C++ struct/vtable additions require consumers to rebuild; the library still has no stable C++ ABI or disk cache.

`tools/raw_quality_compare.py` verifies the preserved gzip/index baseline, pairs the complete corpus by probe/layout/pattern/phase/level, and refuses changed fixture/ground-truth/Bayer hashes, source fingerprints, source-only graph, native request, analytic scope/selection, ROI coverage or metric structure/denominator. It normalizes only the equivalent v2 implicit/v3 explicit demosaic representation. Candidate reports may contain failed correctness cases; reported gates are recomputed from their measurements/checks and inconsistent declarations reject. Finite nonnegative scores and max/MAE/RMSE ordering are validated. This validates harness evidence, without rerendering or independently authenticating pixels. Report hashes identify both inputs; runtime/build/core-source changes are retained rather than used as pixel-parity failures.

**Policy v1:** `tests/reference/raw/replacement_policy_v1.json` fixes provisional engineering targets before candidate evaluation. Each of eight edge and eight smooth-detail probes must improve the arithmetic mean of its 64 paired RGB RMSE scores by at least **10%**, using edge band/detail interior respectively. Their eight neutral probes must also improve residual-chroma RMSE by 10%: **24 separate improvement gates**, all required. Every reconstruction scope of every characterization case caps RGB and applicable neutral chroma RMSE/max error at **baseline × 1.05 + 1e-6**. Information-losing impulse/aliasing cases retain characterization and regression caps, rather than exact-recovery gates. Existing quantization, observed-site fidelity, flat/affine, signed/headroom and exact tile/ROI correctness remain required. A replacement must use a different algorithm/version. These are initial engineering targets, not statistically established camera-quality or perceptual thresholds; version/review any revision rather than tuning silently to a candidate.

**Verified:** full native report `build-msvc-release/raw-quality-explicit-bilinear-v3.json` passed 2,368/2,368 correctness cases. Pairing against `tests/reference/raw/bilinear_baseline_v3.json.gz` returned **2,368 pairs; parity=True; replacement=False**, with no regression or correctness failure. All rendered float32 hashes, metrics, acceptance and tile/ROI checks match the original evidence exactly. The original gzip/index files remain untouched. The comparison record is `build-msvc-release/raw-quality-policy-parity-v1.json`. Regenerate on another machine:

```sh
python tools/raw_quality_harness.py build-msvc-release build-msvc-release/raw-quality-explicit-bilinear-v3.json --build-description "Windows x64; MSVC 19.40.33812; Ninja Release /O2 /DNDEBUG /EHsc; Python 3.9.13; OpenMP enabled; explicit demosaic policy"
python tools/raw_quality_compare.py tests/reference/raw/bilinear_baseline_v3.json.gz build-msvc-release/raw-quality-explicit-bilinear-v3.json build-msvc-release/raw-quality-policy-parity-v1.json --require parity
```

Default `--require replacement` exits 1 for a failed replacement gate; `--require parity` exits 0 for the verified unchanged policy/pixels. Both results are saved. Six comparator tests cover known 20% arithmetic improvements, regression caps, order independence, equivalent legacy/explicit identity, mismatched/duplicate/incomplete/nonfinite evidence, false correctness and inconsistent policy/header. Altered metrics are test data only, not measured candidate quality. New C++ policy tests cover v1/v2/v3 parity, mixed history save/restore, runtime policy verification and cache reuse. The Python RAW session suite now has ten tests, including dict-copy ownership and synchronous/queued/history rejection. Reconfigured/rebuilt optimized default/core-only/LCMS trees; CTest passed **17/17, 8/8, 9/9**, including existing 22 scalar/evidence and seven native quality cases. Existing LittleCMS C5033 warnings remain.

`git diff --check` and new-file whitespace checks passed. Candidate report SHA-256: `e950f19bbf5664b29d2872c85d95091b8f0a3ee84d01b2be27dc254e1837ed23`. Original baseline gzip SHA-256 remains `64c82786da4ccaee86bb51f082cda877276adde89d632c2703905b1185e08d5d`. `graphify update .` refreshed the local code graph (1,317 nodes/3,223 edges); the baseline index/policy JSON data have no AST nodes, and generated output remains locally excluded.

The next three fixture steps and baseline preservation are implemented in `tools/raw_quality_harness.py`. ### RAW slanted-edge continuation — 2026-09-30

The independent quality harness now adds eight neutral/chromatic slanted-edge probes: four-native-pixel linear transitions and hard steps in two orientations. In active-local integer pixel-center coordinates, the lines are `x - 0.25*y - 26.375 = 0` and `x + 4*y - 128.375 = 0`; both pass through `(32.375, 24)`. Signed perpendicular distance divides the line expression by `hypot(1, slope)`. Linear transition uses `clamp(0.5 + distance/4, 0, 1)`; the hard step chooses the high endpoint at distance ≥0. Endpoints retain signed shadows/headroom. All parameters and coordinate/tie rules are saved in each case.

The reducer accepts an exact-shape boolean selection intersected with its rectangular scope, preserves fixed-order float64 accumulation and rejects invalid/nonfinite samples even outside the selection. Edge-band selection is analytic `abs(distance) <= 2`, inclusive, independent of renderer output and sensor offsets. Reports retain whole and one-pixel-interior scores and add edge-band RGB max/MAE/RMSE plus applicable neutral residual chroma. Selected pixel counts are **196** for the near-vertical line and **260** for the near-horizontal line, with selection hashes recorded. The full edge band includes active-area borders.

Edge quality is characterization: analytical scope, reconstruction threshold and analytical gate are **null**. Observed-site quantization/fidelity and exact tile/ROI gates remain required. Summary distinguishes analytical and characterization cases; a passed correctness report makes no edge quality claim. Report schema/generator versions advanced from 1 to **2**, while metric formula version remains **1** and native bilinear algorithm identity remains `bilinear-reference-v1`.

**Verification:** 15 standard-library tests and five native integration cases include independently hand-calculated signed distances/transition points, hard-step ties, inclusive/empty bands, mask denominators/chroma, invalid/nonfinite selection handling, shifted-layout invariance, report repeatability and explicit characterization policy. Existing scalar/RAW integration and core regression cases remain covered. No C++ or build configuration changed, so the previous optimized MSVC/Python 3.9.13 builds were reused; `ctest --test-dir <tree> --output-on-failure` passed **15/15** for `build-msvc-release`, **6/6** for `build-core-release`, **7/7** for `build-lcms-release`.

### Owned RAW session continuation — 2026-09-30

RawSession(bayer, width, height, metadata=None, *, cache_bytes=67108864, workers=1, max_pending=8) copies decoded native-endian uint16 samples and fixed sensor metadata once, validates through RawImage, and memoizes the canonical source fingerprint before retaining the RawUnpackNode. Metadata accepts stride, pattern/CFA phase, active-area and uniform/four-site black/white levels; unknown fields, recipe controls and non-integer scalar metadata reject. source_info returns copied sensor dimensions, normalized stride, pattern/phase, active-area/site-level tuples and manifest ID/kind/fingerprint. Input-buffer or metadata-dict mutation has no effect; source inspection remains available after close.

Each recipe is independent and supports WB gains, exposure, optional caller-calibrated camera matrix/working-space target, tone/output policy and ROI/tile/mip/quality requests. It builds explicit current-processing operations and preserves one-shot RAW stage order: demosaic → WB → camera-linear exposure → optional camera-to-working → output. Native-final defaults use the active sensor rectangle; reduced defaults start at zero with ceil(active_extent/scale). Sensor metadata/budgets/unknown recipe keys and RAW recipe geometry reject. Calibrated saved manifests can apply supported scene-linear crop/orientation/resize and other graph edits, while saved requests remain strictly ROI/tile/mip/quality-only. Reduced source-only camera/unmanaged/ICC/legacy-fixed paths retain their gates.

The binding now uses shared SessionState/SessionObject internally, with generic source-node ownership and optional RAW metadata. Manifest request defaults rebase reduced output to zero instead of dividing a nonzero native origin. History render ownership accepts decoded-Bayer source records with no working_space; the core graph remains responsible for domains. RawSession reuses the existing source/cache/job/export/manifest/footprint/history APIs. Jobs retain graph/source/cache/scheduler handles across session deletion; explicit parent close cancels its jobs. History pins immutable source bindings and shares the cache with its own lazy scheduler/groups, so parent close/deletion does not invalidate retained history. History JSON and graph manifests contain source fingerprints/edits, not Bayer pixel assets; restore requires equivalent supplied sources. RAW source replacement/multi-RAW Python graph ownership remain absent.

**Verification:** VS 2022/v143 Release default Python 3.13.14/OpenMP CTest passed **13/13**, Python/OpenMP OFF **5/5**, and LittleCMS ON/Python/OpenMP OFF **6/6**. Commands: cmake --build build --config Release and ctest --test-dir build -C Release --output-on-failure, repeated for build-core/build-lcms with the existing normalized child environment. The extended PythonRawSession suite then passed all **ten** repetitions via ctest --test-dir build -C Release --output-on-failure --repeat until-fail:10 -R RawEnginePythonRawSessionTests. Nine cases cover both working spaces/all Bayer patterns/phases against the independent native calibration/reduction oracle, exact one-shot/saved/ROI/tiled/scheduled bytes, metadata ownership/defaults/source binding, signed/headroom fixtures, empty results/progress, warm and revised WB/exposure/calibration/tone cache reuse, zero/tight budgets and concurrent clearing, native/reduced sensor footprints, typed saved geometry and history with independent output extents, save/restore/navigation/retention, queued job source ownership after buffer/session deletion, dropped-job cancellation, invalid latest replacement, queue limits/timeouts/close and recovery. Existing raster/manifest/history/preview suites passed; the final native legacy-output/calibrated saved-replay checks also passed in the nine-case RAW session suite. No new benchmark, representative RAW quality, decoder, Adobe, release-binary or packaging claim follows.

### Earlier RAW preview continuation — 2026-09-30

**Contract:** mip 1/2 at Preview quality uses direct clipped 2×2/4×4 averages of native demosaiced, white-balanced, calibrated scene-linear float32 RGB, accumulated in double and returned as float32. Output dimensions are ceil(active_width/scale) × ceil(active_height/scale), with zero-based preview coordinates; footprint origins are (active_x + scale*x, active_y + scale*y). Edge groups divide by actual sample count. Mip 2 averages native pixels directly. Native-final requests retain sensor coordinates and the existing stage order. The compatibility GraphRecipe applies camera-linear exposure before calibration, so that exposure is part of the native anchor input; typed graphs can place scene-linear exposure after calibration. Tone, clipping and encoding run after reduction. CFA phase and site levels always use original sensor coordinates. This reference differs from averaging encoded final output.

CameraToWorkingNode accepts optional native_input_bounds as its third constructor argument; supplying a valid nonempty extent enables reduced preview while preserving the two-argument native-only behavior. ImageGraph RAW and explicit camera_to_working edit operations supply the active/native extent. Node::input_level returns native at this anchor; required_source_regions composes the mapped sensor groups and the clipped one-pixel RAW demosaic halo. Renderer, scheduler and planner consistently rebase reduced output even when native bounds have nonzero origins. Scene-linear point edits, working conversion, reduced-pixel blur and sRGB output propagate the level. Crop/orientation/resize replace the averaging anchor with their own native-output contract; two RAW branches report both original sensor footprints. Cache identity separates native/mip levels and invalidates changed WB/calibration while retaining upstream preview tiles for late tone edits.

Python render accepts mip 1/2 only with quality="preview", a valid camera_to_xyz_d50 matrix and output_mode="srgb-preview". Default preview ROI covers the zero-based reduced active area; explicit ROI/tile sizes use reduced pixels. Source copying and option validation precede GIL release. At this preview milestone RAW was one-shot; the owned RAW session continuation above adds manifest/jobs/history ownership. RAW one-shot geometry, mip-0 preview, reduced Final, uncalibrated/unmanaged/ICC output and legacy fixed-chain manifest preview remain unsupported.

**Verification:** VS 2022/v143 Release default Python 3.13.14/OpenMP CTest passed **12/12**, Python/OpenMP OFF **5/5**, and LittleCMS ON/Python/OpenMP OFF **6/6**. Commands: cmake --build build --config Release and ctest --test-dir build -C Release --output-on-failure, repeated for build-core/build-lcms. MSBuild child environments normalize PATH/Path keys via the bundled Python launcher; Store Python launches use approved sandbox escalation. After the final extended native tests, RawEngineRawPreviewTests passed again in all three trees; after the final Python method documentation and single-pass ROI parsing cleanup, rebuilt rawengine_native and reran PythonRawPreview/PythonSmoke (2/2). Existing LittleCMS C5033 header warnings remain.

Each new reference suite covers 160 combinations of both working spaces, four Bayer patterns, four CFA phases and five zero/nonzero/odd/narrow/one-pixel active areas at both mips, with padded stride and distinct site levels. Native tests compare exact independent averages of calibrated native float32 pixels, exact tiled/ROI/scheduled parity, source halo mapping, signed/headroom retention, geometry/blur/two-source composition, replay, warm reuse and WB/calibration/tone revisions. Python independently computes native Bayer normalization/demosaic/WB/exposure/calibration (Gauss-Jordan matrix inverse), averages those floats and uses the established raster output path as the reference (1e-6 tolerance); it verifies retained native output, exact tiled/ROI parity, coordinate defaults, empty ROIs, strict gates and recovery. No timing, representative image-quality, production demosaic, RAW-file decode, release-binary audit or Adobe measurement is claimed. Native transient footprint tiles remain outside the cache budget.

Commands: cmake --build build --config Release and ctest --test-dir build -C Release --output-on-failure, repeated for build-core and build-lcms. Mip 1/2 now propagate through WorkingToSrgbNode, ToneCurveNode on linear sRGB, and SrgbEncodeNode. Source reduction occurs before nonlinear tone/clipping/encoding. A padded signed/overrange 7×5 fixture in both ProPhoto/D50 and Rec.2020/D65 matches native-resolution rendering of independently averaged linear source pixels (1e-6 tolerance), has exact full/tiled/cropped parity, correct sRGB descriptors and bounds, preserves blur halos, and matches scheduled output. Tests verify warm cache reuse, mip/native-final isolation, upstream reuse after a tone-shoulder revision and rejection of unsupported levels. RAW, ICC import/display and unmanaged tone/output reduced paths remain unsupported.

**Preview/final consistency contract:** compare reduced output against final rendering of a direct equal-weight 2×2/4×4 linear-source reduction, normalizing clipped edge footprints by actual sample count. This reference passes for both working spaces. It intentionally differs from averaging an already tone-mapped/encoded full-resolution image: on this synthetic high-contrast fixture, maximum encoded-channel differences were 0.435732/0.510320 at mip 1/2 for ProPhoto and 0.426053/0.521021 for Rec.2020. These figures characterize operation ordering on this fixture; they are not natural-image quality limits or Adobe measurements. Blur radius is measured in reduced pixels, so spatial previews also need a separately defined final-quality comparison.

### Python preview/session continuation — 2026-09-30

RawEnginePython.cpp now parses typed raster mip/quality requests with reduced viewport defaults and explicit unsupported-level errors. Existing native calls retain defaults and output tuple shape. RAW remains mip 0 final. Native work releases the GIL only after Python options and source bytes have been copied. RasterSession(rgb, width, height, working_space, row_stride_pixels=0, cache_bytes=67108864) copies/validates/fingerprints the source once; render(options) constructs supported versioned unary edit operations and shares a bounded cache across complete independent recipes. cache_stats() exposes entries/used_bytes/hits/misses/budget_bytes; clear_cache() removes entries and resets counters. Budget excludes source, transient buffers and returned bytes. No async jobs, cancellation, generic graph editing or ICC adapter is exposed in Python.

### Async Python continuation — 2026-09-30

RasterSession now lazily creates a bounded TileScheduler with configurable workers=1 and max_pending=8. submit and submit_latest return RenderJob objects with done, cancel, progress and repeatable result(timeout) methods. Native graph/source/cache/scheduler handles survive session deletion; explicit close rejects new work and cancels outstanding async requests. Dropping an unfinished job requests cancellation. Native waits and final scheduler destruction release the GIL; workers never call Python. Completed job result buffers are outside the tile-cache budget. Module-owned heap types and the RenderCancelled exception have module-state GC traversal/cleanup.

TileScheduler::submit_latest accepts an optional caller cancellation token in both overloads, preserving scheduler-created tokens by default. Blocking native probe tests verify caller cancellation and supersession settle queued latest futures without rendering obsolete tiles. Python tests verify timed waits, native/mip parity in both spaces, concurrent/repeated results, exact tile progress, running/queued cancellation, queue capacity with same-group replacement, group isolation, invalid replacement rejection, close, deletion and recovery. VS Release CTest passed **6/6** default, **3/3** core-only and **4/4** LittleCMS. Command ctest --test-dir build -C Release --output-on-failure --repeat until-fail:10 -R "PythonAsyncTests|EditGraphTests" passed all ten repeats of both suites. No new performance or Adobe measurements.

### Crop and coordinate-level continuation — 2026-09-30

CropNode/rawengine.crop (schema 1, current processing) validate an integer nonempty rectangle in scene-linear working input, translate native pixels and rebase output coordinates to zero. ExecutableEditGraph separates original source_bounds() from output_bounds(); renderer/scheduler clients use output_bounds(). Planning tracks each registered stage's input extent and Node::input_level, delegated by cached wrappers. Disabled crops retain upstream geometry. Native and reduced crop/blur/nested chains map to the original source without confusing cropped and upstream edges.

**Reduced crop contract:** mip 1/2 requests direct clipped 2×2/4×4 native upstream footprints anchored at the crop origin, divides by actual sample count at crop edges, and applies downstream edits at the reduced level. This equals native crop extraction into a new scene-linear source followed by reduction. Operations before the final crop execute natively; blur after it measures radius in reduced pixels. The last nested crop supplies the anchor. Reduction requires upstream raster-preview support, so RAW demosaic and ICC source gates remain. This can differ from cropping a reduced full-image preview. Current implementation materializes requested native footprint tiles; cache budgets exclude transient tiles and source storage.

Python render_raster, RasterSession.render/submit/submit_latest accept crop=(x,y,width,height) in original native source coordinates before exposure/tone/encoding. Defaults/ROI use cropped output dimensions at the selected mip; buffers/stride remain original. Crops are per-call; omission/None restores full geometry. RAW one-shot rejects crop. The async scheduler receives transformed output bounds and progress totals describe the cropped output. Optional Python build now requires 3.9+ for module-associated heap-type APIs; current runtime verified 3.13.14, with other versions still a packaging/CI gate.

VS Release CTest passed **6/6** default, **3/3** Python/OpenMP OFF and **4/4** LittleCMS/Python/OpenMP OFF after final changes. Python preview suite now includes a sixth crop/reference/sync/async case with odd offset/extent, clipped reduced ROI, per-call reset and invalid geometry/type tests. Async tests also reject invalid crop replacements without superseding valid queued work.

### Multi-input graph continuation — 2026-09-30

LinearMixNode/rawengine.linear_mix (schema 1) provides a bounded two-input reference: named base/layer ports, amount in [0, 1], matching native extents and linear ProPhoto/Rec.2020 descriptors. It computes (1 - amount) * base + amount * layer without clipping, alpha or masks; endpoints reproduce the corresponding input exactly. Native final and supported mip-1/2 preview execute both branches; disabled operations bypass to base. Both upstream signatures participate in output cache identity. A distinct source boundary retains each manifest source ID even if records share one runtime node without caching.

ExecutableEditGraph::required_source_regions(roi, level) returns reachable source-ID-to-native-rectangle mappings, follows crop anchors/blur halos and per-stage levels, and unions shared-source footprints into conservative bounding rectangles. Revisited node/level regions merge during iterative traversal. Singular required_source_region and source_bounds reject outputs depending on multiple source IDs; single-source DAGs retain their existing helpers. Renderers/schedulers continue to use output_bounds. General masks/blend modes, alpha, disjoint footprint lists and generic Python manifest editing remain absent.

### Resize, Python geometry and benchmark continuation — 2026-09-30

Historical native slice: its reduced-resize restrictions were lifted by the area/resize-preview continuation below.

ResizeNode/rawengine.resize (schema 1) adds a bounded native-final scene-linear reference resampler: positive integer width/height, filter nearest or bilinear, zero-based output bounds, pixel-center mapping (output + 0.5) * input_extent / output_extent - 0.5 and clamped input-edge centers. Nearest half positions round upward. Bilinear uses adjacent samples with double intermediate arithmetic and float32 output. Signed/headroom values remain; identity resize reproduces input exactly. Input planning covers interpolation footprints and composes through crop, blur and mix paths; size/filter participate in cache identity. Disabled resize retains its upstream extent. These filters have no antialias prefilter; production downsampling and reduced-level resize remain open quality gates. Enabled resize rejects mip/preview requests explicitly.

Python render_raster and RasterSession render/submit/submit_latest accept resize=(width,height) and optional resize_filter (default bilinear). Geometry order is crop then resize then exposure/tone/output; source buffer dimensions/stride remain original, while ROI defaults/progress use resized output bounds. Options are per-call; None/omission removes resize. A filter requires resize. Invalid sizes/types/filters/quality reject before queue replacement. RAW one-shot rejects raster resize. Native processing and async waits retain GIL-release/ownership semantics.

### Area filter and resize-preview continuation — 2026-09-30

ResizeFilter::Area and manifest/Python filter area integrate piecewise-constant source pixel cells over each output pixel footprint. Axis boundaries are output * input_extent / output_extent and (output + 1) * input_extent / output_extent; overlap products normalize by footprint area. uint64 rational boundaries exclude zero-overlap neighbors exactly, including near uint32 limits; double accumulation produces float32 values. This box-area reference supports both down/up sampling, identity, odd dimensions and one-pixel extents. Nearest/bilinear semantics/defaults are unchanged; sharper production reconstruction and representative-image evaluation remain open.

**Resize preview contract:** all three filters accept mip 1/2 Preview when their upstream chain supports raster preview and native final. Resize anchors native upstream execution, materializes the requested native resized scene-linear float32 footprint, then averages direct clipped 2×2/4×4 groups using actual edge sample counts. Downstream point/tone/encoding stages operate on the reduced result; blur after resize uses reduced pixels, while pre-resize blur runs natively. This equals separately materializing native resized linear output and reducing it, and can differ from resizing directly to reduced dimensions or averaging encoded output. The last crop/resize anchor determines the grid. Source planning composes native sampling footprints through crop/blur/mix; caches keep output levels distinct. RAW/ICC reduced gates and unsupported mip/quality rejection remain. Native temporary resized tiles are outside the cache budget; this reference method computes native resized samples before averaging and is not a direct low-cost preview shortcut.

Python one-shot/session sync/async/latest calls now accept area and resize mip-1/2 sRGB previews with resized/reduced ROI defaults and output-tile progress. The benchmark accepts area and explicit --mip 1/2 with resize (default still native mip 0 when resize is supplied). Recipe geometry order and source-buffer stride/ownership remain unchanged. Timed waits, repeatable results, cancellation, queue replacement and failed-call recovery retain their contracts.

VS v143 Release cmake --build build --config Release, repeated for build-core/build-lcms, and ctest --test-dir each-tree -C Release --output-on-failure passed **6/6**, **3/3**, and **4/4** after final fixtures. Both working spaces pass an independent continuous-overlap native oracle (1e-5 float tolerance on the signed/overrange ramp), exact preview equality against independently averaged native float32 output, native/reduced full/tiled/ROI/scheduler/replay parity, odd-edge normalization, cache level isolation/revisions, separate-source area footprints and crop/blur/mix composition. Constant/ramp/impulse mean/energy checks pass. For the defined 9×9 checker reduced to 3×3, area alternates 4/9 and 5/9 (contrast 1/9), while bilinear alternates 0 and 1 (contrast 1); this characterizes that fixture, not a universal quality score. Exact boundary/uint32 planning checks pass without giant allocations. RAW rejects resized preview; the LittleCMS suite verifies native area resize of ICC-imported working pixels and reduced ICC rejection. Python suites (seven preview and seven async cases) cover all filters/spaces/crops at native/mip-1/mip-2, independent linear-source/output reference (1e-6 encoded tolerance), ROI/progress, empty previews, warm/late-tone reuse, native/reduced running cancellation and invalid replacement preservation. ### Orientation and Python geometry continuation — 2026-09-30

OrientationNode/rawengine.orientation (schema 1) implements exact clockwise quarter turns (integer quarter_turns 0..3), then boolean horizontal/vertical flips in rotated coordinates. Odd turns swap width/height; output is zero-based. Native rendering copies signed/overrange float32 pixels, including signed zero, without interpolation. Integer inverse mapping gives exact source rectangles and composes through crop, resize, blur and mix branches, including nonzero input origins. Parameters participate in cache identity; disabled orientation bypasses its input and preserves its bounds.

Mip-1/2 previews average direct 2×2/4×4 transformed native pixels with actual odd-edge sample counts. The last crop/orientation/resize supplies the native reduction anchor; rotating an already-reduced odd-sized grid is a different operation. RAW/ICC reduced-input gates remain unchanged. Python one-shot, RasterSession sync and submit/submit_latest accept rotate=0/90/180/270 clockwise and strict boolean flip_horizontal/flip_vertical, with per-call defaults. Recipe order is original-source crop → rotation → rotated-coordinate flips → resize → exposure/output. Output ROI, default extents and progress use the resulting geometry; RAW calls reject raster geometry options.

### Python saved-manifest continuation — 2026-09-30

Added RasterSession.source_info(), export_manifest(options), render_manifest(json, request), submit_manifest(json, request, priority=...) and submit_manifest_latest(group, json, request, priority=...). Export writes format-v2 JSON; the shared core reader also supports its explicit v1 migration. Binding checks the complete saved source record/fingerprint and runtime metadata. Graphs may branch from the session's one owned source, including linear mix and box blur. Additional source bindings, unsupported operations/schema/processing versions, invalid domains/edges, masks/compositing metadata and ICC transforms fail closed. Manifest request dictionaries accept only ROI, tile_size, mip and quality; saved output executes directly without an added output chain. Native scene-linear bytes remain scene-linear. Default request bounds/progress follow transformed output extents. JSON parsing, graph binding, rendering and waits release the GIL after copying Python input. Jobs retain source/cache/node ownership; recipe and manifest latest jobs share groups. Failed validation cannot supersede a valid existing job. Source metadata remains readable after close; execution/export rejects new work after close.

Full default suite passed, then the expanded manifest suite passed separately; core-only and LCMS configurations were rebuilt and passed after those additions. git diff --check passed. No new benchmark, Adobe measurement or release-binary audit was performed.

Environment recovery: sandbox execution exposed duplicate Path/PATH entries, causing MSBuild MSB6001; normalized child environment keys with the bundled Python 3.12 runtime. The sandbox could not launch installed Windows Store Python 3.13, so approved build/test execution used that existing interpreter outside the sandbox. Reconfigured each existing tree with -DPython3_EXECUTABLE=C:/Program Files/WindowsApps/PythonSoftwareFoundation.Python.3.13_3.13.3824.0_x64__qbz5n2kfra8p0/python3.13.exe. Build command: cmake --build <tree> --config Release; checks: ctest --test-dir <tree> -C Release --output-on-failure, plus -R RawEnginePythonManifestTests for expanded cases. The default native module retains its cp313 ABI. Changes remain local/uncommitted.

### Python multiple-owned-source continuation — 2026-09-30

Added RasterGraphSession(sources, *, cache_bytes, workers, max_pending), source_info(), export_manifest(source_id), replace_source(source_id, source_spec), saved-manifest sync/async/latest methods and existing cache/close controls. Sources are a strict dictionary of 1–64 lowercase UUIDs to explicit native-endian contiguous float32 RGB specs: rgb, width, height, working_space and optional row_stride_pixels. Buffers are copied before releasing the GIL, then validated/fingerprinted as immutable native sources. The manifest may declare a subset of owned sources; each declared record must match exactly. Missing/unknown bindings reject. Skeleton export writes all current source records and a selected source-only output. Mixed spaces/extents require explicit conversion/geometry before linear mix. No implicit display/output chain is added.

Replacement validates a new copy before atomically swapping one existing binding under a source mutex. Bound graphs/jobs retain their immutable source snapshots; new bindings reject stale saved fingerprints. Cache source/branch signatures preserve unchanged branches and invalidate downstream changed paths without clearing the shared LRU. Failed replacement preserves existing state/jobs; concurrent export-to-bind races may reject a stale record rather than produce a mixed frame. Both RasterSession and RasterGraphSession expose required_source_regions(json, request) as reachable source-ID-to-native-rectangle tuples, including composed crop/orientation/resize/blur and disabled mix's base-only dependency. Source metadata remains readable after close; replacement/export/inspection/execution rejects new work after close. RasterSession recipe and one-source APIs remain compatible. Owned source copies, retained old job sources, graph allocations, transient buffers and Python result bytes remain outside the cache budget. History and source add/remove are absent.

Verification: VS 2022/v143 Release, default Python 3.13/OpenMP CTest **8/8**, Python/OpenMP OFF **3/3**, LittleCMS ON with Python/OpenMP OFF **4/4**. Seven new RawEnginePythonMultiSourceTests cases independently verify signed/headroom mixes in both spaces at native/mip-1/2, odd/padded/differently sized sources, exact full/tile/ROI/job parity, endpoint/disabled mix, subset binding, independent crop/quarter-turn/area-resize references and exact per-source footprints, explicit working-space conversion and extent/domain rejection, source metadata/identity/spec failures, unchanged-branch cache reuse (one hit/two misses after replacing the other source), stale-manifest rejection and failed replacement recovery, concurrent replacements/coherent frames, queue/supersession/cancellation/close, repeatable waiters/progress and job/source lifetimes. The single-source manifest suite also checks source-footprint inspection. Existing preview/async suites each retain eight cases and the saved-manifest suite six cases. No new benchmark, Adobe reference or release audit was performed.

Commands: cmake --build <tree> --config Release and ctest --test-dir <tree> -C Release --output-on-failure for build/build-core/build-lcms; selected new suite also ran with -R RawEnginePythonMultiSourceTests before final full regression. Existing environment workaround normalizes child environment keys through bundled Python 3.12 to avoid duplicate Path/PATH and uses approved installed Python 3.13 execution outside the sandbox. Reconfigured existing trees with the previously documented explicit Python3_EXECUTABLE path; the native module remains cp313. git diff --check passed. No commit or push.

### Immutable manifest history continuation — 2026-09-30

EditHistory in EditGraph.hpp/.cpp owns fixed declared source bindings and immutable EditRevision snapshots containing canonical manifest JSON plus executable graphs. Initial revision is ID 1; commits fully validate before publication, assign monotonic positive IDs without reuse, discard redo after branching and evict oldest retained states until both count and aggregate canonical UTF-8 manifest-byte limits hold. Defaults: 64 revisions/4 MiB; permitted limits: 1–100000 revisions and 1–16 MiB. A new revision must fit by itself. Failed commits preserve IDs, cursor, redo and retained states. Graph/source/cache allocations, transient copies, external snapshots and jobs/results are outside the manifest-text budget; cache retention follows its existing independent LRU budget. C++ current/revision/comparison snapshots remain usable after navigation, truncation, eviction or history destruction. Missing IDs/navigation raise EditRevisionUnavailable.

History-format-1 JSON stores canonical manifest strings, limits, retained IDs, current selection and next ID; it embeds no pixels. Save/replay is deterministic in this implementation, preserves redo/ID gaps, validates every supported graph and source identity on restore, and rejects unknown history fields/versions, malformed order/IDs and budget violations. Input/output JSON is capped at 16 MiB including escaping/envelope overhead. Source identity records are fixed across commits; source versions cannot silently rebind past revisions. Pinned in-memory source nodes are retained by histories independently from live parent source replacement; restoring saved history still requires matching supplied/current fingerprints. Binary source/mask asset persistence and source-version archival remain absent.

Python RasterSession and RasterGraphSession expose history(manifest, *, max_revisions, max_manifest_bytes) and restore_history(saved). EditHistory provides commit, undo/redo, stats, snapshot, save, render, submit/submit_latest, compare and close. Rendering captures one immutable selected revision; compare atomically captures two retained IDs and returns separate native/reduced results with independent geometry defaults and explicit output domains. History shares the parent's tile cache, inherits worker/queue budgets for its own lazy scheduler, and has independent cursor, close state and viewport groups. Parent close/deletion/replacement cannot alter history pixels. Bound jobs survive revision truncation/eviction; failed submissions do not supersede valid group jobs. Close rejects new mutation/render/comparison/job work and cancels unfinished jobs, while stats/snapshot/save remain readable. Parsing, validation, rendering and waits release the GIL. Unavailable revisions/navigation map to IndexError; invalid render requests preserve ValueError behavior.

Verification: VS 2022/v143 Release rebuild and CTest default Python 3.13/OpenMP **10/10**, Python/OpenMP OFF **4/4**, optional LittleCMS with Python/OpenMP OFF **5/5**. New C++ history suite covers navigation/geometry, atomic comparison snapshots, canonical save/restore with nonterminal cursor, redo truncation/ID gaps, count/byte eviction, failed commit/binding/budget recovery, immutable external snapshots, concurrent commits and scheduled source/job lifetimes. Seven Python history cases cover exact native/mip-1/2 geometry/reference and job parity in both working spaces, count/byte budgets and oversized rejection, malformed history/version/source/ID/request validation, warm and late-tone cache reuse through undo/redo (one hit/two misses for the new tone/output path), independent histories, pinned multiple-source replacement/restore semantics, jobs surviving truncation/eviction, queue failures/cancellation/close, concurrent commits, repeatable waiters and history/parent destruction. Existing preview/async suites retain eight cases each, saved-manifest six and multi-source seven. No new benchmark, Adobe measurement or production release audit was performed.

Commands: cmake --build <tree> --config Release and ctest --test-dir <tree> -C Release --output-on-failure for build/build-core/build-lcms; focused tests used -R History before final regression. Existing normalized child-environment and explicit installed Python 3.13 workaround was reused. One core-only LNK1114 overwrite/access error recovered on retry without source changes. Existing LittleCMS C5033 register warnings remain. git diff --check passed. No commit or push.

### Previous verified runs

Current Windows machine reports an Intel Core i9-14900K; RAM, display resolution and power mode were not verified. VS 2022 Preview MSVC **19.40.33812** and Python **3.9.13** were used. The Codex shell omitted standard Windows profile/temp variables, so the build commands ran after restoring `USERPROFILE`, `HOMEDRIVE`, `HOMEPATH`, `APPDATA`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `SystemRoot`, `windir`, `TEMP` and `TMP`, then calling Preview `VsDevCmd.bat -arch=x64 -host_arch=x64`. Its Ninja CMake cache otherwise left `CMAKE_CXX_FLAGS_RELEASE` empty; the verified Release builds explicitly set `/O2 /DNDEBUG /EHsc` (and `/O2 /DNDEBUG` for the LittleCMS C build). The resulting `build-msvc-release` core + Python + benchmarks passed CTest **4/4**, `build-core-release` with Python/OpenMP OFF passed **3/3**, and `build-lcms-release` with LittleCMS ON and Python/OpenMP OFF passed **4/4** after unary ROI planning. The edit-graph/concurrency test passed 30 consecutive repeats after supersession. Tests cover SHA-256 against an independent Python digest, padding-independent visible-sample identity, runtime source mismatch rejection, late-edit cache parity/reuse/eviction, in-flight clear, cancellation, queued priority, group-scoped queued/running request supersession and concurrent ROIs. The 7×5 signed/overrange scene-linear blur fixture matches untiled output at tile sizes 1/2/3/8 and in a cropped ROI, replays from serialized manifest, and invalidates cached output when radius changes. A two-blur-plus-exposure chain composes both halos into the expected source rectangle and matches full output at tile sizes 1/2/3; legacy RAW replay plans its one-pixel demosaic halo. A core-only local stage install included `RawEngine.dll`/`.lib`, all three public headers (including `TileScheduler.hpp`), targets and own license. The Python and ICC entry points were retained. No Adobe capture or LittleCMS staged binary/license audit was run this time.

For `1af87f6`, the same optimized MSVC Release configurations rebuilt and CTest passed **4/4**, **3/3**, and **4/4**, respectively. Added checks compare typed native final render to the legacy overload, preserve tile partitioning, exercise typed scheduler and latest-group requests, reject unsupported reduced mip/preview quality before rendering or queueing, and prove generic cache entries separate native final, native preview, and reduced preview levels. The direct cache test uses a native node only to inspect key separation; it does not claim valid reduced pixels. The public renderer still executes only mip 0 final quality. No new benchmark or Adobe measurement was run for this small API step.

For `5ad317c`, the same optimized MSVC Release configurations rebuilt and CTest passed **4/4**, **3/3**, and **4/4**. A padded 7×5 scene-linear raster fixture validates numeric 2×2 averages, signed/overrange preservation and odd-edge normalization; full and tile sizes 1/2/3 produce exactly equal pixel vectors, and cropped ROI pixels equal the corresponding full preview pixels. A source-only executable graph with a shared `TileCache` reuses reduced tiles on warm render, keeps native final tiles separate, and matches a scheduled reduced request. Unsupported mip 2, mip 1 final quality, out-of-bounds reduced ROI and a reduced request through an exposure node reject explicitly. No performance benchmark, Adobe capture or staged LittleCMS binary audit was run for this step.

For `b9be3aa`, `2958d50` and `1136e52`, the optimized MSVC core+Python+benchmarks build passed CTest **4/4** after each code slice; the final core-only and LittleCMS configurations passed **3/3** and **4/4**. Mip 1 preview runs exposure and working-space conversion after source reduction, including warm/revised per-level cache tests, native-final isolation and full/tiled/ROI parity. Reduced box blur uses a radius/halo in reduced pixels; a source→blur→exposure→conversion chain maps its halo back to original pixels and matches direct output without tiled seams, while a radius edit invalidates output. Mip 2 averages direct clipped 4×4 original-source footprints; a padded 7×5 fixture checks numerical values that differ from recursive equal-weight mip-1 averaging, exact tile parity, scheduler results, cache separation, edited graph parity and clipped ROI mapping. No benchmark, Adobe capture or staged LittleCMS binary/license audit was run for these slices.

Run the following inside a configured MSVC developer environment (the three build directories are Git-ignored):

```sh
cmake -S . -B build-msvc-release -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release -DCMAKE_CXX_FLAGS_RELEASE="/O2 /DNDEBUG /EHsc" -DRAWENGINE_BUILD_BENCHMARK=ON
cmake --build build-msvc-release
ctest --test-dir build-msvc-release --output-on-failure
build-msvc-release/RawEngineCacheBenchmark.exe
cmake -S . -B build-core-release -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release -DCMAKE_CXX_FLAGS_RELEASE="/O2 /DNDEBUG /EHsc" -DRAWENGINE_BUILD_PYTHON=OFF -DRAWENGINE_USE_OPENMP=OFF
cmake --build build-core-release
ctest --test-dir build-core-release --output-on-failure
cmake -S . -B build-lcms-release -G Ninja -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release -DCMAKE_CXX_FLAGS_RELEASE="/O2 /DNDEBUG /EHsc" -DCMAKE_C_FLAGS_RELEASE="/O2 /DNDEBUG" -DRAWENGINE_WITH_LCMS=ON -DRAWENGINE_BUILD_PYTHON=OFF -DRAWENGINE_USE_OPENMP=OFF -DRAWENGINE_BUILD_BENCHMARK=ON
cmake --build build-lcms-release
ctest --test-dir build-lcms-release --output-on-failure
```

Three independent `RawEngineCacheBenchmark.exe` processes on this i9 machine, synthetic 2048×1536 scene-linear raster, 1024×768 streaming ROI, 256-pixel tiles and 48 MiB cache, produced medians: initial source fingerprint **100.957 ms**, cold render **12.948 ms**, warm render **1.361 ms** (each process's median of five), late-edit graph construction **0.062 ms**, and first render after changing tone shoulder **6.822 ms**. Each run ended with 63 entries, 49,561,344 charged bytes, 72 hits and 72 misses. This is a narrow process-local point-operation cache measurement on synthetic pixels, not a fit preview, RAW decode, production demosaic or export result. It must not be compared as a speedup to the earlier i5 measurements under different inputs/configurations.
